Let's design final code around this family + refinement:
- Function `make_beta_sequence(alpha, beta, n)`:
  t = (np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
  a = t^{-alpha} * (1-t)^{-beta}; handle α=0: t^0=1; if t=0 no. Add small epsilon for t? Half-grid avoids.
  If alpha=1? not; clip.
  Normalize to sum=1.
  Return a.
- Search alpha,beta: Use a coarse grid maybe α=0:0.1:0.8, β=0:0.1:0.8-α. Evaluate score with fast. Keep best. Then local Nelder-Mead `scipy.optimize.minimize` with method Nelder-Mead or COBYLA, bounds. Since objective not smooth and 2D, Nelder-Mead robust. Or use nested loops with golden? We can do grid + random + Powell. Time small.
- Also consider reverse (swap alpha,beta) but score should same? A reversed sequence's convolution reversed, max same, sum same. So symmetric in α,β? Actually f and reverse have same max convolution (reversed A² coefficients), score same. So no need, but include.
- We can also use a generalized shape: `a_i = (t_i + c0)^(-alpha) * (1 - t_i + c1)^(-beta) * exp(γ * t_i)`? But beta with α,β maybe enough. Add linear base? We can search over more params? Too many. Maybe include `a = exp( a1*t + a2*t^2 + a3*cos(π t) + ...)` with clipping, but nonneg.

Now refinement full sequence:
- Starting from best family sampled at target N (maybe 2048 or 2535). Use fast internal score. If score e.g. 1.45, done. If e.g. 1.6, local gradient may lower.
- Implement `refine_sequence(a, time_budget)`.
  * Normalize a sum=1. n fixed.
  * x = log(a + eps) - log(a+eps).mean? Actually x = log(a / S) (sum1), center.
  * Adam: m,v arrays.
  * For iter while time:
    - compute b via FFT using x->a? We can update a from softmax of x? But x starting from a; after update x, `a = softmax(x)` changes sum1. To preserve scale, ok. We can instead work with a directly in log domain: a = exp(x); normalize by sum. If x updated, renormalize. Simpler: Maintain x (not softmax), a = exp(x) / sum(exp(x)). For gradient, use softmax Jacobian. Start x=log(a) - mean.
    - `a = softmax(x_clipped)` each iteration. But x and a relation. We can update x with Adam, then recompute a. For gradient, compute a, b, cache A_fft.
    - Compute b, maxb, tau.
    - weights = exp((b - maxb)/tau); normalize.

def refine_sequence(a, budget_s, seed=123, target=1.503):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n=a.size; a=clip nonneg; S=sum; if S<... return a
    a/=S
    x=np.log(a)+1e-8; x-=x.mean()
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a)
    # maybe exact at best
    L=...
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); step=0
    lr=0.05; beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; eps=1e-8
    tau_frac=0.25; min_frac=0.005
    stagnation=0
    x_current=x.copy(); a_current=a.copy(); score_current=best_score
    while time < deadline:
       # every 200 or stagnation>... perturb best?
       a = softmax(x_current)? if using x as log unnormalized: a=np.exp(x_current); a/=a.sum()
       b, fa = convolve_with_fftcache(a,L)
       maxb=b.max()
       tau=max(min_frac*maxb, maxb*tau_frac)
       z=(b-maxb)/tau
       # for stability, subtract max? bmax. weights normalized
       # if z range large, underflow for inactive okay.
       w=np.exp(z); wsum=w.sum(); if not finite or <=0: w=np.zeros; w[active]=1/active
       w/=wsum
       # corr via FFT: W=rfft(w,L); c=irfft(W*np.conj(fa), L)[:n]
       c = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(w,L) * np.conj(fa), L)[:n]
       ga=2*c
       # softmax grad
       dot=np.dot(a,ga)
       gx=a*(ga-dot)
       # Adam
       m=beta1*m+(1-beta1)*gx
       v=beta2*v+(1-beta2)*gx*gx
       mhat=m/(1-beta1**step); vhat=v/(1-beta2**step)
       x_new=x_current - lr*mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+eps)
       # to keep scale, center
       x_new -= x_new.mean()
       # clip
       x_new=np.clip(x_new, -30,30)
       # evaluate
       a_test=np.exp(x_new); S=a_test.sum(); a_test/=S
       score_test=score_fast(a_test)
       # occasionally exact direct? maybe every 100.
       if finite and score_test < best_score:
           best=a_test; best_score=score_test; best_x=x_new
           stagnation=0
           # reduce tau_frac maybe
           tau_frac=max(min_frac, tau_frac*0.98)
       else:
           # If worse than current by more than 5%, revert
           if score_test > score_current * 1.05:
               # revert to previous x and skip update? 
               x_current = x_prev? We didn't store x_prev? store before update.
           else:
               x_current = x_new; score_current=score_test
           stagnation +=1
       step+=1
       if stagnation>200: reset x_current=best_x; score_current=score_fast(best); tau_frac=0.1; add noise; stagnation=0
       if time... maybe check target break
    return best

Full Adam details need careful to avoid wasting time. Let's design a function `optimize_log_start(a_start, max_time, seed)` that returns best a. We can call with several starts.

Potential scaling in Adam: The softmax gradient might be small; use x*log? Maybe optimize a directly with multiplicative updates, which may be more stable and avoid softmax cancellation? Let's compare.
Direct log-domain without enforcing sum each step: x=log a, update x, then normalize a=exp(x)/sum. This is equivalent softmax. Fine.
Gradient of F(a) on normalized a. Since F homogeneous degree0, gradient can be computed as above. Using softmax Jacobian: `gx = a * (ga - a·ga)`. But `a·ga` might be large; subtracting can lead to cancellation. We can instead work with a constrained descent: choose update in tangent space: `a_new = a*(1 - eta * ga)` then project to sum1/nonneg (normalize). This is multiplicative gradient without softmax Jacobian and often stable. Let's analyze. For n=2 uniform with smooth weight including edges, multiplicative update can break symmetry and move. Because `a_i *= exp(-eta g_i)` changes ratios. If g symmetric, no move; if small asymmetry, yes. We can maintain log a: x ← x - eta * ga; normalize log by mean. This is simpler and probably better. The objective scale-invariant; applying same factor to all ga doesn't matter. We can subtract mean of ga before x update to preserve scale: x_new = x - lr * (ga - mean(ga)). Since a=exp(x)/sum. This avoids softmax Jacobian. Gradient of F wrt log a with scale invariance is effectively ga - mean(ga). This should work. Let's use this direct log gradient. It is like multiplicative weight update.
- At a, compute smooth gradient ga=2 corr(w,a). Then update x = log(a) - lr*(ga - ga.mean()) (center). Then normalize (a=exp(x) sum1). Clip x. This is simple. Need line search.
Let's test n=2 uniform with smooth finite. b=[.25,.5,.25]. weights maybe [w0,w1,w2]. ga=2corr(w,a). For a=[.5,.5]. w symmetric if exact symmetric weights -> ga=[ga,ga], no move. If start random asymmetric, move. Good.
Temp schedule: If uniform seed, add tiny random noise to break symmetry before optimizing.

Line search: We can use exact score after each update. If score improves, accept and maybe increase lr, else try half step. Since Adam? We can use plain gradient with potential momentum. Let's implement Adam on x update direction `d = ga - mean(ga)`. Use Adam to adapt, not raw. But direct log gradient is enough. Do:
```
m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*d
v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*d*d
x_new = x - lr * mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps)
```
This is Adam on log. It preserves scale by centering d (or x after). The objective derivative wrt log at scale? We already centered; fine.
Set lr=0.1? Since ga ~? With normalized S=1, a~1/n, ga~1/n, Adam normalizes, step in x maybe lr. x range small. Try lr=0.2. To be safe, backtracking.
Backtracking: If new_score > current_score * (1+1e-6), try step factor 0.5,0.25 by recomputing x_new = x - factor*adam_step. Choose if finite and <= current_score + tolerance. If none, keep x and reduce lr. If improves, maybe increase lr up to 0.5.
This handles nonconvex.
- Stagnation: if no best improvement for 500 iter, reset x to best_x with random perturbation (e.g., add Gaussian to random 10% of entries scale 0.05) and set lr lower/higher, tau_frac reset.
- Multiple starts: We'll call this optimizer for maybe 3 seeds within deadline; each with own time slice if best not target.

Can we get fewer than 1000s? We'll allocate:
- Family search: 60s max (or until target)
- Full optimizer start1 on best family: 300s
- Full optimizer start2 on random asymmetric: 250s
- Full optimizer start3 on perturbed current/other: 250s

Top-level functions:
```
def _fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(a>0, a, 0.0)
    S = a.sum()
    if S <= 0: return np.inf
    n=a.size
    m=2*n-1
    L=1
    while L<m: L <<=1
    f=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b=np.fft.irfft(f*f, L, norm=None) # numpy default unnormalized? rfft/irfft inverse has factor 1/L, so convolution correct.
    b = b[:m]
    # numerical negative small
    if b.size: maxb = float(np.max(b))
    if maxb <0: maxb=0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * maxb / (S*S))
```
Use cache L? We can pass L to avoid recompute. For repeated same n. Define `_fft_len(n)`.
```
def _convolve_fft(a,L):
  fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
  b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,L)[:L?]
```
Need exactly 2n-1; but if L larger, first m good.

Score with smooth and gradient:
```
def _smooth_grad(a, L, tau_frac, tau_min):
   n=a.size; S=a.sum(); maybe normalize? We'll ensure a sum1.
   fa=rfft(a,L); b=irfft(fa*fa,L)[:2n-1]
   bmax=b.max()
   if bmax<=0: tau=1e-12
   tau=max(bmax*tau_frac, tau_min)
   z=(b-bmax)/tau
   # z can be very negative; exp underflow to0.
   w=np.exp(z)
   wsum=w.sum()
   if not finite or wsum<=0: active=np.argwhere(b>=maxb*(1-1e-12)); w=zeros; w[active]=1/len
   else w/=wsum
   W=rfft(w,L)
   c=irfft(W*np.conj(fa), L)[:n]
   ga=2*c
   # Remove roundoff negatives? ga can be negative? It is derivative; weights positive, a positive, c positive? Convolution of positive weights and a positive, should be positive. Due FFT small negative, clip? We can set c[c<0 & > -1e-15]=0.
   c = np.maximum(c,0.0) maybe if tiny.
   return ga, bmax
```
Because weights, a nonnegative, c nonnegative.

Optimizer:
```
def _grsv_optimize(a, time_budget, seed=0, target=1.5):
    rng=...
    n=a.size; L=_fft_len(n)
    a=np.maximum(0, np.asarray(a)); S=a.sum(); if S<...: a=np.ones(n); 
    a/=S; x=np.log(a); x-=x.mean()
    best=a.copy(); best_score=_fast_score(a)
    # exact if time? maybe skip
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); tstep=0
    lr=0.15; tau_frac=0.4; min_tau_frac=0.03
    stagn=0; no_improve=0; revert_count=0
    prev_score=best_score; prev_x=x.copy()
    small=1e-12
    start_time=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start_time+time_budget
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        # compute grad
        ga,bmax = _smooth_grad(a,L,tau_frac,tau_min=1e-12)
        d=ga - ga.mean()
        # if all d tiny? (uniform) perturb x with noise
        if np.max(np.abs(d)) < 1e-15:
           idx=rng.integers(0,n,size=n//10)
           x[idx] += rng.normal(0,0.1, fig?) # break symmetry
        m=0.9*m+0.1*d
        v=0.999*v+0.001*d*d
        tstep +=1
        mhat=m/(1-0.9**tstep); vhat=v/(1-0.999**tstep)
        step = lr * mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
        # line search factors
        accepted=False
        for fac in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125):
           x_new = x - fac*step
           x_new -= x_new.mean()
           x_new = np.clip(x_new, -30,30)
           a_new = np.exp(x_new); Sum=a_new.sum(); a_new /= Sum
           sc = _fast_score(a_new)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc <= prev_score + 1e-12:
                x=x_new; a=a_new; prev_score=sc; accepted=True
                if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                   best=a_new.copy(); best_score=sc; no_improve=0
                   # anneal tau, increase lr slightly
                   tau_frac=max(min_tau_frac, tau_frac*0.98)
                   lr = min(0.4, lr*1.01)
                else:
                   no_improve +=1
                break
        if not accepted:
           # choose best among tried? We didn't store. Could just revert.
           revert_count +=1
           if revert_count%20==0: lr *=0.7
           no_improve +=1
        # periodic reset/perturb if too much no_improve
        if no_improve > 500:
            # reset to best with random direction
            x = np.log(best) - np.log(best).mean()
            # add random low-frequency noise
            t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
            mode=rng.integers(1,6)
            amp=rng.uniform(0.05,0.3)
            x += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*mode*t + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
            # maybe random small
            x += rng.normal(0,0.02,n)
            x-=x.mean(); x=np.clip(x,-30,30)
            a=np.exp(x); a/=:; prev_score=_fast_score(a)
            no_improve=0; lr=0.1; tau_frac=0.3; m*=0; v*=0; regret? best unchanged
        # Occasionally exact check if target
        if no_improve < something and time%? We can't modulo easy; maybe every 200 iterations.
        if int(no_improve)%200==0 and time...:
           exact=evaluate_sequence(best.tolist())
           if finite and exact < best_score: best_score=exact
    # final exact
    exact=evaluate_sequence(best.tolist())
    if finite and exact < best_score: best_score=exact
    # maybe scale best to sum 1? already
    return best, best_score
```
Problem: The line search condition `sc <= prev_score + 1e-12` may accept tiny increases, causing drift up but okay. It may get stuck if first step equal due smooth; but perturb. For nonconvex, a monotone path may be needed; Adam with line search should.

Potential issue: Adam moment from d = ga - mean(ga). For log-unnormalized, if d has positive/negative, step is normalized by RMS; learning rate effectively absolute x update ~lr. lr=0.15 means each iteration can change logs by ~0.15, decent. If objective changes quickly, line search reduces.

Should we include exact evaluate_sequence inside loop for best every e.g., 1000 iters? It is O(n²) direct; maybe one per 100s fine. But fast score is FFT same as exact max (within error). No need until final. Because target tolerance maybe 1e-6; FFT error ~1e-12 maybe okay. We'll exact final.

But if FFT score is slightly under direct due numerical underestimating max, we might think target achieved but exact not. At final, if exact >1.503, we may need keep searching. We can final loop: while time>5s and exact>target, maybe run more optimize with slightly stricter target. But exact only after full runs. def _make_beta(alpha,beta,n):

   left=t; right=1-t
   a = np.exp(-alpha*np.log(left) - beta*np.log(right))

Histogram search:
```
def _hist_search(n, initial_w, time_limit, K=16, nstarts=4):
   best=initial_w; best_score=fastscore(hist(initial))
   for s in range(nstarts):
      if time left <3: break
      w0 = best + rng.normal(0,0.5,K) (or pattern)
      def obj_func(wl):
         a=_make_histogram(wl,n,K); return _fft_score(a)
      # Use Nelder-Mead with maxfun limited by time? Can't callback time easily in obj. Set maxiter=2000.
      try res=_opt.minimize(obj_func, w0, method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter':2000, 'xatol':1e-3, 'fatol':1e-6, 'disp':False})
      except: pass
      update
   exact update
```
But `obj_func` nested closure? Rules: "Make all helper functions top level and have no closures from function nesting. Don't use any lambda functions." So cannot define `obj_func` inside `_hist_search`. Need implement objective as top-level? But objective needs n,K and maybe time. We can define top-level `_hist_objective(w, state)`? Scipy minimize passes only x; can't pass state unless use global variables or define a top-level function that uses global variables. We can set a module-level dictionary/global `_HIST_STATE`. But global mutable okay? Top-level function no closure. For example:
```
_HIST_STATE = {'n':0,'K':0}
def _hist_objective(w):
   n=_HIST_STATE['n']; K=_HIST_STATE['K']
   a=_make_histogram(w,n,K); return _fft_score(a)
```

def _random_block_sequence(rng, n, max_blocks=6):
    K = int(rng.integers(2, max_blocks+1))
    bounds = np.linspace(0,1,K+1)
    # maybe perturb inner bounds
    if K>2:
       bounds[1:-1] = np.sort(rng.random(K-1))
    amps = np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.7,K))
    # ensure not symmetric? okay
    idx = (bounds * n).round().astype(int)
    idx[0]=0; idx[-1]=n
    a=np.zeros(n)
    for j in range(K):
      if idx[j+1]>idx[j]: a[idx[j]:idx[j+1]]=amps[j]
    return _normalize(a)
```

def _refine_sequence(a0, time_budget, seed=1, target=1.503):
    rng=default_rng(seed)
    a=a0 copy; n=a.size; L=_fft_len(n)
    a=_normalize_floor(a)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=_score_unit(a,L)
    x=np.log(a + FLOOR); x-=x.mean(); x=np.clip(x,-30,30)
    # ensure a from x? We can set a from x for consistency
    a=_exp_to_unit(x)
    prev_score=_score_unit(a,L); prev_x=x.copy()
    m=zeros; v=zeros; tstep=0; lr=0.2; tau_frac=0.25; min_tau=0.04
    no_improve=0; rejects=0; consec_rej=0
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_budget
    t=np.linspace(0,1,n) maybe for noise
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        ga,bmax = _gradient_smooth_unit(a,L,tau_frac)
        if not finite bmax: break
        d=ga - ga.mean()
        if np.max(np.abs(d))<1e-15:
            # add tiny noise
            idx=rng.integers(0,n,n//5)
            x[idx]+=rng.normal(0,0.1,len(idx)); x-=x.mean(); x=clip
            a=_exp_to_unit(x); prev_score=_score_unit(a,L)
            continue
        m=...; v=...; tstep+=1
        step=lr*mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
        # Recompute x_new with step
        if consec_rej > 10: lr *= 0.8; consec_rej=0
        x_new = x - step
        x_new -= x_new.mean(); x_new = np.clip(x_new,-30,30)
        a_new = _exp_to_unit(x_new)
        sc_new = _score_unit(a_new,L)
        if np.isfinite(sc_new):
            if sc_new <= prev_score + 1e-12:
                x=x_new; a=a_new; prev_score=sc_new; converge? 
                if sc_new < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best=a_new.copy(); best_score=sc_new; no_improve=0; best_x=x.copy()
                    tau_frac=max(min_tau, tau_frac*0.99)
                    # lr slight up
                    lr=min(0.6,lr*1.01)
                else:
                    no_improve +=1
                consec_rej=0
            else:
                no_improve +=1; consec_rej +=1; lr = max(0.01, lr*0.9)
                # if prev_score much worse than best, reset soon
        else:
            # bad, revert, reset?
            no_improve+=1; consec_rej+=10; lr*=0.5
        if no_improve > 600 or (no_improve>200 and prev_score > best_score*1.02):
            # reset to best with noise
            x=best_x.copy()
            amp=rng.uniform(0.1,0.4)
            mode=int(rng.integers(1,7))
            phase=float(rng.uniform(0,2*math.pi))
            x += amp*np.sin(2*math.pi*mode*t + phase)
            # random blobs
            nbed=int(max(10,n//20))
            idx=rng.choice(n, size=nbed, replace=False)
            x[idx] += rng.normal(0,0.2,nbed)
            x-=x.mean(); x=np.clip(x,-30,30)
            a=_exp_to_unit(x); prev_score=_score_unit(a,L)
            no_improve=0; consec_rej=0; lr=0.15; tau_frac=0.25; m*=0; v*=0
    # final exact? avoid inside? maybe evaluate once
    exact=_exact_score(best)
    if finite and exact < true? We can't update true easily return exact.
    return best, min(best_score, exact if finite)
```

def _exact_score(a):
    try:
       L = a.size
       # ensure list floats no inf
       arr = np.asarray(a)
       if not np.isfinite(arr).all(): return np.inf
       # keep nonnegative
       arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
       val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr])
       return float(val)
    except Exception:
       return np.inf
```

def _use_current_parent(n):
    try:
       parent=np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float)
    except Exception: return None
    parent=np.maximum(parent,0); parent=parent[np.isfinite(parent)]
    if parent.size==0: return None
    if parent.size != n:
        x_old=np.linspace(0,1,parent.size); x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
        parent=np.interp(x_new, x_old, parent)
    parent=np.maximum(parent,1e-18); parent/=parent.sum()
    return parent

Let's write `_fft_len` efficiently:
```
def _fft_len(n):
    m=2*n-1
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```

`_score_unit(a,L)`:
```

`_gradient_smooth_unit`: Return `ga,bmax`. Use `L` power. Code:
```
def _gradient_smooth_unit(a, L, tau_frac):
    n=a.size
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,L)[:2*n-1]
    b=np.where(b>0, b, 0.0)
    bmax=float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    if bmax <= 0 or not np.isfinite(bmax): return 2.0*np.ones(n), 0.0
    bmean=float(np.mean(b))
    tau=max(bmax*tau_frac, 1e-18)
    z=(b-bmax)/tau
    # suppress -inf? underflow fine
    ws=np.exp(z)
    sw=ws.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw<=0:
        ws=np.zeros(n? 2n-1); ws[int(np.argmax(b))]=1.0
    else: ws/=sw
    tl = bmean + 0.45*(bmax-bmean)
    mask = b >= tl
    if not np.any(mask): mask[int(np.argmax(b))]=True
    wa = mask.astype(float); wa /= wa.sum()
    w = 0.7*wa + 0.3*ws
    W=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(W*np.conj(fa), L)[:n]
    c = np.where(c > 0, c, 0.0)
    ga = 2.0*c
    return ga,bmax
```
Maybe active weight 0.7, smooth 0.3. If tau large, ws near uniform; but active part keeps.
`mask` length 2n-1, `wa` float. Good.

Let's tune random Fourier:
```
def _random_fourier_sequence(rng,n):
    t=(arange(n)+0.5)/n
    x=np.zeros(n)
    nterms=int(rng.integers(2,7))
    for i in range(nterms):
       f= float(rng.integers(1,20))
       c= float(rng.normal(0,1.2))
       s= float(rng.normal(0,1.2))
       ph= float(rng.uniform(0,2*pi))
       x += c*np.cos(2*pi*f*t+ph) + s*np.sin(2*pi*f*t+ph)
    x += float(rng.normal(0,0.5))
    a=np.exp(0.4*x)
    return _normalize(a)
```
Fluctuations in log up to few e, nonnegative, can create structured balance.

Random block sequences with 4-8 blocks can create discontinuities; combination of both seeds. Good.

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative height sequence minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / sum(a)^2
while respecting the sanity checks of evaluate_sequence.

The method is a hybrid:
1. A broad cheap family search (steps, power laws, randomized blocks /
   fourier-like positive polynomials) produces diverse seeds.
2. A full-length multiplicative-gradient optimizer is run from the best
   seeds. The objective is smoothed by a log-sum-exp of the convolution
   coefficients plus an active-band weight, and the sequence is kept on the
   probability simplex in log-space with backtrack-style accept/reject.
3. If time remains and the score is still above the target, the best sequence
   is resampled to a longer length and refined again.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math


def _fft_len(n):
    """Next power of two large enough for full linear convolution."""
    m = 2 * int(n) - 1
    if m <= 0:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize(a):
    """Return a finite non-negative normalized vector (sum 1)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        if a.size == 0:
            a = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
        return a / float(a.size)

    a = a / s
    # tiny floor to keep logs safe; effect is negligible
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-18)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / a.size
    return a / s


def _exp_to_unit(x):
    """Convert log-heights to a normalized non-negative vector."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    # remove roundoff/numerical extremes
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x = x - float(np.max(x))
    a = np.exp(np.clip(x, -70.0, 70.0))
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(x.size, dtype=np.float64) / x.size
    return a / s


def _score_unit(a, L):
    """Score assuming a already has sum 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)[: (2 * n - 1)]
    b = np.where(b > 0.0, b, 0.0)
    if b.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    mb = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(mb) or mb < 0.0:
        mb = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * mb)


def _candidate_key(item):
    return item[0]


def _insert_candidate(cands, a, L, max_items=12):
    """Insert candidate if its fast score is finite."""
    a = _normalize(a)
    s = _score_unit(a, L)
    if not np.isfinite(s) or S >= 1e12:
        return
    cands.append((float(s), a.copy()))
    cands.sort(key=_candidate_key)
    if len(cands) > max_items:
        del cands[max_items:]


def _make_uniform(n):
    return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)


def _make_step(p, v, n):
    """First fraction p has height 1, remainder has height v."""
    a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    k = int(max(0, min(n, int(round(p * n)))))
    if 0 < k < n:
        a[k:] = float(v)
    return _normalize(a)


def _make_powerlaw(alpha, beta, n):
    """
    Discrete analogue of x^{-alpha} (1-x)^{-beta} on a half grid.
    This produces asymmetric U-shaped or one-sided singular-looking shapes.
    """
    t = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    lt = np.log(np.maximum(t, 1e-300))
    rt = np.log(np.maximum(1.0 - t, 1e-300))
    a = np.exp(-float(alpha) * lt - float(beta) * rt)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    return _normalize(a)


def _make_linear(c, s, n):
    """a = 1 + c*(1-t)^s, a simple one-sided family."""
    t = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    a = 1.0 + float(c) * np.power(np.maximum(1.0 - t, 1e-12), float(s))
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    return _normalize(a)


def _random_block_sequence(rng, n):
    """Random nonnegative piecewise-constant sequence."""
    K = int(rng.integers(2, 7))
    raw = np.linspace(0, n, K + 1, dtype=np.float64).round().astype(int)
    raw[0] = 0
    raw[-1] = int(n)

    jitter = int(max(1, n // 25))
    if K > 2:
        raw[1:-1] = np.clip(
            raw[1:-1] + rng.integers(-jitter, jitter + 1, size=K - 1),
            1,
            int(n) - 1,
        )
    idx = np.unique(np.sort(raw))
    if idx[0] != 0:
        idx = np.concatenate(([0], idx))
    if idx[-1] != int(n):
        idx = np.concatenate((idx, [int(n)]))

    if idx.size < 2:
        return _make_uniform(n)

    amps = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.7, size=idx.size - 1))
    a = np.zeros(int(n), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(idx.size - 1):
        i0 = int(idx[i])
        i1 = int(idx[i + 1])
        if i1 > i0:
            a[i0:i1] = float(amps[i])
    return _normalize(a)


def _random_fourier_sequence(rng, n):
    """Random positive log-Fourier sequence."""
    t = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    x = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    terms = int(rng.integers(2, 7))
    for _ in range(terms):
        f = float(rng.integers(1, 20))
        c = float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.2))
        s = float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.2))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi))
        th = 2.0 * math.pi * f * t + ph
        x += c * np.cos(th) + s * np.sin(th)
    x += float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5))
    a = np.exp(0.35 * x)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    return _normalize(a)


def _current_parent(n):
    """Optionally use the provided global height_sequence_1 as a seed."""
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    except Exception:
        return None

    parent = np.where(np.isfinite(parent), parent, 0.0)
    parent = np.maximum(parent, 0.0)
    if parent.size == 0:
        return None
    s = float(parent.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return None

    if parent.size != int(n):
        if parent.size < 2:
            val = s / parent.size
            out = np.full(int(n), max(val, 1e-12), dtype=np.float64)
            return _normalize(out)
        x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, parent.size)
        x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(n))
        parent = np.interp(x_new, x_old, parent)

    return _normalize(parent)


def _resample_sequence(a, m):
    """Interpolate to a new length."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a = _normalize(a)
    if a.size == 0:
        return _make_uniform(m)
    if a.size == int(m):
        return a.copy()
    if a.size < 2:
        return np.full(int(m), max(a.sum(), 1e-12), dtype=np.float64)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(m))
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    return _normalize(out)


def _gradient_smooth_unit(a, L, tau_frac):
    """
    Gradient of a smoothed illumination of max(conv(a,a)):
        log-sum-exp on all coefficients + active-band emphasis.
    Returns gradient with respect to a and the current max coefficient.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = a.size
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)[: (2 * n - 1)]
    b = np.where(b > 0.0, b, 0.0)

    if b.size == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), 0.0

    bmax = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(bmax) or bmax <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), 0.0

    bmean = float(np.mean(b))
    tau = max(bmax * float(tau_frac), 1e-18)

    z = (b - bmax) / tau
    ws = np.exp(z)
    sw = float(ws.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <= 0.0:
        ws = np.zeros_like(b)
        ws[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
    else:
        ws /= sw

    # emphasize a band near the maximum, but smoothing keeps secondary
    # coefficients in the picture.
    tl = bmean + 0.45 * (bmax - bmean)
    mask = b >= tl
    if not np.any(mask):
        mask[int(np.argmax(b))] = True
    wa = mask.astype(np.float64)
    wa_sum = float(wa.sum())
    if wa_sum <= 0.0:
        wa[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        wa_sum = 1.0
    wa /= wa_sum

    w = 0.70 * wa + 0.30 * ws
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(W * np.conj(fa), L)[:n]
    c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
    ga = 2.0 * c
    return ga, bmax


def _refine_sequence(a0, time_budget, seed=123):
    """
    Full-length log-space gradient descent with restarts.
    Returns (sequence, fast_score).
    """
    a = _normalize(a0)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        n = 2048
        a = _make_uniform(n)

    L = _fft_len(n)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(1.0, float(time_budget))

    x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-18))
    x -= float(x.mean())
    x = np.clip(x, -30.0, 30.0)
    a = _exp_to_unit(x)

    best = a.copy()
    best_x = x.copy()
    best_score = _score_unit(a, L)
    prev_score = best_score

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    step_t = 0

    lr = 0.18
    tau_frac = 0.25
    min_tau_frac = 0.04
    no_improve = 0
    reject_streak = 0
    last_print = t0
    tgrid = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        ga, bmax = _gradient_smooth_unit(a, L, tau_frac)
        if not np.isfinite(bmax) or bmax <= 0.0:
            break

        # keep previous score synchronized with current a
        prev_score = float(2.0 * n * bmax)

        d = ga - float(ga.mean())
        if float(np.max(np.abs(d))) < 1e-16:
            # Perfectly constant gradient: break symmetry.
            idx = rng.integers(0, n, size=int(max(16, n // 10)))
            x[idx] += rng.normal(0.0, 0.12, size=idx.size)
            x -= float(x.mean())
            x = np.clip(x, -30.0, 30.0)
            a = _exp_to_unit(x)
            prev_score = _score_unit(a, L)
            continue

        step_t += 1
        beta1 = 0.90
        beta2 = 0.95

        m = beta1 * m + (1.0 - beta1) * d
        v = beta2 * v + (1.0 - beta2) * (d * d)

        mhat = m / (1.0 - (beta1 ** step_t))
        vhat = v / (1.0 - (beta2 ** step_t))
        step = lr * mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-8)

        x_new = x - step
        x_new -= float(x_new.mean())
        x_new = np.clip(x_new, -30.0, 30.0)
        a_new = _exp_to_unit(x_new)
        sc_new = _score_unit(a_new, L)

        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new <= prev_score + 1e-11:
            x = x_new
            a = a_new
            prev_score = float(sc_new)
            reject_streak = 0
            lr = min(0.6, lr * 1.02)

            if sc_new < best_score - 1e-13:
                best = a_new.copy()
                best_x = x_new.copy()
                best_score = float(sc_new)
                no_improve = 0
                tau_frac = max(min_tau_frac, tau_frac * 0.995)
            else:
                no_improve += 1
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            reject_streak += 1
            if reject_streak % 8 == 0:
                lr = max(0.02, lr * 0.75)

        if no_improve > 700 or (no_improve > 250 and prev_score > best_score * 1.03):
            # Reset to the best restart point and inject structure.
            x = best_x.copy()

            amp = float(rng.uniform(0.08, 0.45))
            mode = int(rng.integers(1, 8))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi))
            x += amp * np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * mode * tgrid + phase)

            # random block perturbations
            nb = int(max(16, min(512, n // 16)))
            blk = rng.integers(0, n, size=nb)
            x[blk] += rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, size=nb)

            x -= float(x.mean())
            x = np.clip(x, -30.0, 30.0)
            a = _exp_to_unit(x)
            prev_score = _score_unit(a, L)

            no_improve = 0
            reject_streak = 0
            lr = 0.15
            tau_frac = 0.25
            m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

        now = time.monotonic()
        if now - last_print > 5.0:
            print(
                f"    refine n={n} best_fast={best_score:.6f} "
                f"cur={prev_score:.6f} tau={tau_frac:.3f} lr={lr:.3f}"
            )
            last_print = now

    return best, best_score


def _exact_score(a):
    """Call the authoritative evaluator, if available."""
    try:
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
        a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
        val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in a])
        return float(val)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def _diverse_seeds(n, time_limit, seed):
    """
    Build a small list of diverse normalized candidate sequences.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    L = _fft_len(n)
    cands = []
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(1.0, float(time_limit))

    # uniform and current parent
    a = _make_uniform(n)
    _insert_candidate(cands, a, L)
    par = _current_parent(n)
    if par is not None:
        _insert_candidate(cands, par, L)

    # simple one-step families
    ps = np.linspace(0.05, 0.95, 18)
    vs = np.concatenate(
        (
            np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, 22),
            np.linspace(1.0, 2.5, 12),
        )
    )
    for p in ps:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        for v in vs:
            _insert_candidate(cands, _make_step(p, float(v), n), L)

    # power-law / beta-like shapes
    alphas = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, 18)
    betas = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, 18)
    for al in alphas:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        for be in betas:
            _insert_candidate(cands, _make_powerlaw(float(al), float(be), n), L)

    # simple one-sided curves
    curs = [0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0]
    coef = [-0.9, -0.5, -0.2, 0.0, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0]
    for c in coef:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        for s in curs:
            _insert_candidate(cands, _make_linear(c, s, n), L)

    # random structured blocks / positive log-fourier
    count = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        count += 1
        if count % 3 == 0:
            a = _random_fourier_sequence(rng, n)
        else:
            a = _random_block_sequence(rng, n)
        _insert_candidate(cands, a, L)
        # do not let seed generation consume everything; the full optimizer
        # gets the remaining budget.
        if count > 4000 and time.monotonic() > t0 + 0.5 * (deadline - t0):
            break

    # Exact-evaluate a few top candidates so ordering is authoritative.
    if cands:
        exact_top = []
        for sc, a in cands[:5]:
            ex = _exact_score(a)
            metric = ex if np.isfinite(ex) else sc
            exact_top.append((metric, a))
        exact_top.sort(key=_candidate_key)
        if exact_top:
            cands = exact_top
            if len(cands) > 12:
                del cands[12:]

    return cands


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=950.0, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search entry point.
    Returns a Python list of non-negative floats.
    """
    try:
        limit = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        limit = 950.0

    # The prompt states a hard 1000 s timeout; reserve a margin.
    limit = max(20.0, min(limit, 950.0))

    t_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t_start + limit - 5.0
    target = 1.503

    # For a 1000 s budget, use a fairly long sequence.  For short budgets,
    # fall back to smaller n to keep each FFT cheap.
    if limit > 600:
        n = 4096
    elif limit > 200:
        n = 2048
    else:
        n = 1024

    print(f"propose_candidate start: budget={limit:.1f}s n={n}")

    # 1) cheap diverse seed search.  Leave the main time to the optimizer.
    family_limit = min(60.0, max(8.0, 0.08 * limit))
    cands = _diverse_seeds(n, family_limit, int(seed))
    if not cands:
        a = _make_uniform(n)
        cands = [(_score_unit(a, _fft_len(n)), a)]

    best_a = cands[0][1].copy()
    best_metric = _exact_score(best_a)
    if not np.isfinite(best_metric):
        best_metric = cands[0][0]

    print(f"seed best metric (exact if available) = {best_metric:.6f}")

    # 2) build refine seeds.
    refine_seeds = []
    for _, a in cands[:4]:
        refine_seeds.append(a.copy())

    par = _current_parent(n)
    if par is not None:
        refine_seeds.append(par)

    rng0 = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) + 991)
    refine_seeds.append(_random_block_sequence(rng0, n))
    refine_seeds.append(_random_fourier_sequence(rng0, n))

    # avoid completely duplicate starts (very cheap test)
    uniq = []
    for a in refine_seeds:
        a = _normalize(a)
        ok = True
        for b in uniq:
            if float(np.max(np.abs(a - b))) < 1e-6:
                ok = False
                break
        if ok:
            uniq.append(a)
    refine_seeds = uniq[:6]

    # 3) run the full optimizer with decreasing time slices.
    fractions = [0.42, 0.26, 0.16, 0.10, 0.06]
    first = True
    for idx, a_start in enumerate(refine_seeds):
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining < 15.0:
            break

        if first:
            frac = fractions[0]
        elif idx < len(fractions):
            frac = fractions[idx]
        else:
            frac = 0.06

        slice_s = max(10.0, frac * remaining)
        slice_s = min(slice_s, remaining - 10.0)
        if slice_s < 10.0:
            break

        print(
            f"refine seed {idx + 1}/{len(refine_seeds)} "
            f"slice={slice_s:.1f}s remaining={remaining:.1f}s"
        )
        res_a, res_fast = _refine_sequence(a_start, slice_s, seed=1000 + 17 * idx)
        ex = _exact_score(res_a)
        metric = ex if np.isfinite(ex) else res_fast

        if np.isfinite(metric) and (not np.isfinite(best_metric) or metric < best_metric - 1e-12):
            best_a = res_a.copy()
            best_metric = float(metric)
            print(f"  improved: metric={best_metric:.7f}")

        first = False

    # 4) If still above target and time is available, resample to a longer
    #    sequence and do another refinement.  Longer n improves the
    #    continuum/discrete approximation and can shave the last small gap.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 180.0 and (not np.isfinite(best_metric) or best_metric > target):
        n2 = min(8192, max(int(best_a.size) * 2, int(best_a.size) + 512))
        if n2 > int(best_a.size):
            a2 = _resample_sequence(best_a, n2)
            slice_s = min(remaining - 20.0, max(60.0, 0.55 * remaining))
            print(f"resample/refine to n={n2} slice={slice_s:.1f}s")
            res_a, res_fast = _refine_sequence(a2, slice_s, seed=4321 + int(seed))
            ex = _exact_score(res_a)
            metric = ex if np.isfinite(ex) else res_fast
            if np.isfinite(metric) and (not np.isfinite(best_metric) or metric < best_metric - 1e-12):
                best_a = res_a.copy()
                best_metric = float(metric)
                print(f"  improved after resample: metric={best_metric:.7f}")

    # 5) Final exact verification (best effort).
    final_ex = _exact_score(best_a)
    if np.isfinite(final_ex):
        print(f"final exact score = {final_ex:.7f}")
    else:
        L = _fft_len(best_a.size)
        print(f"final fast score = {_score_unit(best_a, L):.7f} (eval unavailable?)")

    out = np.asarray(best_a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    if out.size == 0:
        out = np.ones(max(n, 256), dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.01:
        out = out / max(s, 1e-12) * 10.0
    else:
        # keep scale moderate; sum=1 is well inside the evaluator's safe range.
        out = out / s

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
